# Published BEIR numbers on 7 datasets: nDCG@10 and Recall@100

Collected 2026-10-26 for the ragkit head-to-head. Scope: BEIR official **test** split / test qrels of **SciFact, NFCorpus, FiQA-2018, ArguAna, SCIDOCS, TREC-COVID, Quora**. Every number below was read from the URL in its row during this session (no number is from memory). `[INFERENCE]` marks my own reading. "not found" entries are listed in section 6 with what was tried.

How I read things: `read`/`curl` on the URLs in section 1; MTEB per-file JSONs were fetched in bulk from the pinned results-repo commit and parsed by script (`scores.test[0].ndcg_at_10`, `recall_at_100`); Pyserini page values were transcribed by hand and cross-checked against the Anserini regression docs (section 2.2). No model was run or downloaded.

† = the model's own mteb `training_datasets` metadata lists some of these 7 datasets, so its row is not zero-shot (details in section 5, trap T10). Rerankers are two-stage and are not comparable to first-stage rows (trap T9).

## 2. Pyserini BEIR regression: retrieval parameters as documented

### 2.1 What the page and the linked sources say

- The page (S1) lists exactly five systems: BM25 Flat (`bm25-flat`), BM25 MF (`bm25-multifield`), SPLADE++ CoCondenser-EnsembleDistil (`splade-pp-ed`), Contriever FT MS MARCO (`contriever-msmarco`), BGE-base-en-v1.5 Faiss flat (`bge-base-en-v1.5.faiss`). No other system is on that page (read in full, 142 lines of converted text). Its references: [1] Kamalloo et al. arXiv 2306.07471, [2] SPLADE++ SIGIR 2022, [3] Contriever arXiv 2112.09118, [4] C-Pack arXiv 2309.07597.
- The page does **not** state k1/b. Its BM25 commands are `python -m pyserini.search.lucene --threads 16 --batch-size 128 --index beir-v1.0.0-<ds>.flat --topics beir-v1.0.0-<ds>-test --output-format trec --hits 1000 --bm25 --remove-query`; the multifield variant adds `--fields contents=1.0 title=1.0` and uses index `beir-v1.0.0-<ds>.multifield`. Evaluation: `python -m pyserini.eval.trec_eval -c -m ndcg_cut.10 beir-v1.0.0-<ds>-test <run>` and `-c -m recall.100`.
- k1/b defaults, from source I opened: Anserini `SearchCollection.java` `-bm25.k1` default `"0.9"` and `-bm25.b` default `"0.4"` (lines 277-281; file last commit 2026-09-13); Pyserini `_searcher.py` line 583 `def set_bm25(self, k1=float(0.9), b=float(0.4))` and `__main__.py` `--k1`/`--b` options without override. The BEIR paper (S3 section 4) says: "We use Anserini with the default Lucene parameters (k=0.9 and b=0.4). We index the title (if available) and passage as separate fields".
- Analyzer: Anserini `IndexCollection.java` line 55 `DEFAULT_ANALYZER = DefaultEnglishAnalyzer.newDefaultInstance()`, `-stemmer` default `"porter"`, `-keepStopwords` default `false`; `DefaultEnglishAnalyzer.java` line 88-93: "Creates a new instance with all defaults: Porter stemming, Lucene's default stopwords". Kamalloo et al. (S4 section 7.1): "we used Lucene's default analyzer for English".
- Index contents, from the Anserini regression docs (S2, `beir-v1.0.0-scifact.flat.md` and `.multifield.md`, same text for the other datasets) and source:
  - flat: "These experiments index the corpus in a "flat" manner, by concatenating the "title" and "text" into the "contents" field." Code: `BeirFlatCollection.java` line 77-78 `contents = title + "\n" + text`. Index options `-storePositions -storeDocvectors -storeRaw`.
  - multifield: "These experiments index the "title" and "text" fields in corpus separately. At retrieval time, a query is issued across both fields (equally weighted)." Index options add `-fields title`; search adds `-fields contents=1.0 title=1.0`. `BeirMultifieldCollection.java`: "text" maps to `contents`, "title" is its own field.
  - Retrieval depth 1000, `-removeQuery` (drops a hit whose docid equals the query id).
- SciFact index stats (yaml, S2): 5183 documents, 784,591 terms. BM25 SciFact flat 0.6789 / R@100 0.9253 / R@1000 0.9767; multifield 0.6647 / 0.9076 / 0.9800.
- Datasets with no title (FiQA, Quora per BEIR Table 1 "Title ✗"): flat and multifield give identical numbers on the page (FiQA 0.236/0.539 both, Quora 0.789/0.973 both). Kamalloo et al. say the same: queries over a corpus without titles "yielded the same ranking".

### 2.2 Cross-check of my page transcription

I re-fetched the Anserini regression docs for all 7 datasets and 5 configs (`flat`, `multifield`, `splade-pp-ed.onnx`, `splade-v3.onnx`, `bge-base-en-v1.5.parquet.flat.onnx`) and compared with the page values I transcribed (3 decimals). Max absolute difference: BM25 flat 0.0005 (FiQA), BM25 multifield 0.0005 (FiQA): rounding only. SPLADE++ ED 0.0072 (ArguAna: page 0.526 / 0.983 vs Anserini-ONNX 0.5218 / 0.9758) and BGE-base 0.0015 (ArguAna: page 0.636 vs 0.6375): the page encodes with PyTorch via Pyserini, the Anserini docs use ONNX, so these are small implementation differences, not typos. Contriever has no Anserini doc, so it is transcription-only.

Only rows in this file; † rows excluded from the first-stage column.

| Dataset | Best 1st-stage retriever listed (not flagged †) | value | Best reranked/2-stage listed | value |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| SciFact | Conan-embedding-v2 (MTEB) | 0.843 | monoT5 (3B) rerank BM25 top-100 | 0.766 |
| NFCorpus | Conan-embedding-v2 (MTEB) | 0.557 | monoT5 (3B) rerank BM25 top-100 | 0.390 |
| FiQA-2018 | Conan-embedding-v2 (MTEB) | 0.719 | BM25+CE (BEIR paper) | 0.347 |
| ArguAna | Conan-embedding-v2 (MTEB) | 0.778 | BM25+CE (BEIR paper) | 0.311 |
| SCIDOCS | Qwen3-Embedding-8B (MTEB) | 0.327 | BM25+CE (BEIR paper) | 0.166 |
| TREC-COVID | Qwen3-Embedding-8B (MTEB) | 0.950 | RankGPT-4 rerank SPLADE++ ED top-100 | 0.879 |
| Quora | Conan-embedding-v2 (MTEB) | 0.923 | BM25+CE (BEIR paper) | 0.825 |


Caution on this table: Conan-embedding-v2 is not flagged by mteb metadata but is far above every other unflagged model on 5 of 7 datasets, and its metadata resolves to a generic E5-Mistral set [INFERENCE: metadata may be incomplete]. Qwen3-Embedding-8B TREC-COVID 0.950 exceeds every published reranker row in this file; I did not verify why. Treat both as unreplicated.

| 2 | TencentBAC__Conan-embedding-v2 | 0.7119 | mteb meta lists none of 7 (resolves to E5-Mistral set) [INFERENCE: may be incomplete] |
| 3 | Qwen__Qwen3-Embedding-8B | 0.6828 | mteb meta lists none of 7 |
| 4 | infly__inf-retriever-v1 | 0.6750 | † mteb meta lists all 7 |
| 5 | Qwen__Qwen3-Embedding-4B | 0.6717 | mteb meta lists none of 7 |

| 7 | BAAI__bge-en-icl | 0.6557 | not checked |
| 8 | yibinlei__LENS-d8000 | 0.6492 | not checked |
| 9 | Linq-AI-Research__Linq-Embed-Mistral | 0.6436 | not checked |
| 10 | NovaSearch__jasper_en_vision_language_v1 | 0.6434 | not checked |

The rank-1 and rank-4 rows list the evaluated datasets among their own training data (S10), so they are not zero-shot leaders. Several rows come from `external` files (model-card metadata, no harness version): Linq, SFR, NV-Embed-v1, bge-en-icl, LENS, gte-Qwen2-1.5B are all mostly `external`.

## 5. Known comparability traps

Each trap cites a source I opened. `[INFERENCE]` = my reading, not stated by the source.

**T1. nDCG uses linear gain, not 2^rel - 1.** trec_eval `m_ndcg_cut.c`: "Gain values are the relevance values in the qrels file. For now, if you want different gains, change the qrels file appropriately." and the DCG sum is `gain / log2(i + 2)` (lines 27-32, 72-75). BEIR computes nDCG@k through `pytrec_eval` (BEIR paper S3 section 3.3 "we utilize the Python interface of the official TREC evaluation tool"; BEIR `evaluation.py` line 101). Source: https://github.com/usnistgov/trec_eval/blob/master/m_ndcg_cut.c (file date not retrieved; GitHub API rate-limited). Consequence [INFERENCE]: an nDCG implementation with exponential gain gives different numbers on graded qrels (TREC-COVID and NFCorpus are 3-level in S3 Table 1); binary-qrels sets are unaffected.

**T2. Relevance 0 means not relevant.** trec_eval `trec_eval.c` line 186 sets the default `epi.relevance_level = 1`; `m_ndcg_cut.c` line 73 only accumulates `if (gain > 0)`. BEIR's Touché qrels had -2 labels "substituted as zero" (S3 Appendix D.5; Touché is outside our 7). A custom scorer that treats `score >= 0` as relevant would inflate every dataset that ships explicit 0 rows [INFERENCE: which of our 7 ship 0-rows not checked]. Source: https://github.com/usnistgov/trec_eval/blob/master/trec_eval.c

**T3. Identical query/document ids must be dropped (ArguAna, Quora, FiQA).** BEIR `EvaluateRetrieval.evaluate(..., ignore_identical_ids=True)` pops a result when `qid == pid` (lines 73-84, file last commit 2025-06-04; https://github.com/beir-cellar/beir/blob/main/beir/retrieval/evaluation.py). Pyserini does it via `--remove-query` (S1 commands; option default is False in `pyserini/search/lucene/__main__.py` line 152); Anserini `-removeQuery` (S2). The official leaderboard "discard[s]" queries returned among retrieved documents (S4 section 6). MTEB: the base default is `ignore_identical_ids: bool = False` (`mteb/abstasks/retrieval.py` line 96; `_evaluators/retrieval_evaluator.py` lines 131-145) and only `ArguAna`, `FiQA2018`, `QuoraRetrieval` set it to `True` in the task files (S10, main 2026-10-25), none of SciFact/NFCorpus/SCIDOCS/TRECCOVID do. Old result files may predate that flag [INFERENCE; per-file harness versions are in the long list]. Observed spread on ArguAna BM25: 0.315 (S3 Table 2), 0.397 flat / 0.414 multifield (S1, S4). The cause of 0.315 is not stated in S3 [INFERENCE: query not removed].

**T4. TREC-COVID: version, depth and capped recall.** BEIR uses "the July 16, 2020 version of CORD-19 dataset as corpus and the final cumulative judgements" (S3 Appendix D.1), 50 queries, 66,336 judgments (S4 Table 1), so many queries have more than 100 relevant documents. S3 Table 9 reports capped recall (Appendix G: "we cap the recall score (R_cap@k) at k ... if the number of relevant documents for a query [is] greater than k"); BM25 TREC-COVID R@100 is 0.498 there but 0.114 in S4/S1 (uncapped, same dataset), MTEB `recall_at_100` is uncapped (bge-base 0.140, Pyserini 0.141). Which Table 9 cells are capped: the caption says capped values are marked, but the markers were lost in the PDF text conversion; TREC-COVID is the only row whose magnitude shows it [INFERENCE]. Also the judgments are pooled from lexically-biased submissions: S3 section 1 ("strong lexical bias ... We manually annotate the missing relevance judgements ... significant performance improvement for non-lexical approaches") and S5 Limitations. RankGPT's BM25 0.5947 (S7) equals the Anserini flat value 0.5947 (S2), so S7 used the same TREC-COVID corpus and qrels [INFERENCE].

**T5. MTEB harness vs BEIR/Pyserini scorer.** MTEB computes `pytrec_eval` `ndcg_cut.k` and `recall.k` (`mteb/_evaluators/retrieval_metrics.py` lines 603-613), i.e. the same library as BEIR, and `recall_at_100` is uncapped. Differences are in the run, not the metric: query-id handling (T3), the dataset revision (T12), the encoder prompt/instruction, max length. BEIR averages over `len(scores)` where `scores` comes from `evaluator.evaluate(results)` (lines 104-115): queries that are missing from the run are not counted at all, while `trec_eval -c` (Pyserini) "Average[s] over the complete set of queries in the relevance judgements" (`trec_eval.c` line 45). Whether pytrec_eval omits missing queries was not tested [INFERENCE].

**T6. SPLADE-v3 paper reports nDCG\*.** S6 (evaluation-setup paragraph): "nDCG* stands for the nDCG considering only the judged documents (encountered in the retrieved top-k) if the dataset has both positive and negative judgments - otherwise, we use the standard nDCG@10". Which of our 7 datasets count as "has negative judgments" is not stated. The Anserini ONNX run of the same model uses standard nDCG@10 (S2) and differs: TREC-COVID 74.8 (paper) vs 0.7299, ArguAna 50.9 vs 0.4845, FiQA 37.4 vs 0.3798, NFCorpus 35.7 vs 0.3629, SciFact 71.0 vs 0.7140, SCIDOCS 15.8 vs 0.1567, Quora 81.4 vs 0.8141. Do not mix the two.

**T7. "BM25" is not one system.** Same dataset, different published BM25: TREC-COVID 0.595 flat / 0.656 multifield / 0.5947 RankGPT (S1, S3, S7); NFCorpus 0.322 / 0.325 / 0.3075 (S1, S3, S7); ArguAna 0.397 / 0.414 / 0.315. The BEIR paper's text says Anserini k1=0.9,b=0.4 with separate title/text fields, while Kamalloo et al. (S4 section 3.1) write "The original BEIR paper presented a BM25 baseline using Elasticsearch", calling it multi-field. S3 itself also says Elasticsearch was tested on its leaderboard and Anserini "perform[ed] the best". Which engine produced S3 Table 2 is therefore ambiguous in the sources [INFERENCE: values equal Pyserini multifield on 6 of 7 datasets and differ only on ArguAna]. For a model-free ragkit BM25, the like-for-like targets are S1 `bm25-flat` (title+text in one field) and `bm25-multifield`, and they depend on analyzer (Porter + Lucene English stop list), k1=0.9, b=0.4.

**T8. Truncation.** Neural systems in S3 see only "the first 512 word pieces"; BM25 sees the full document. S4 section 7.2 shows MaxP vs FirstP changes TAS-B by about 0.01 nDCG@10 on average (0.414 vs 0.422/0.424); MTEB models truncate at their own max length [INFERENCE: per-model max length not collected].

**T9. Two-stage numbers.** RankGPT (S7) and RankZephyr (S8) rerank a fixed first-stage top-100 (BM25 for S7; BM25 or SPLADE++ ED for S8); RankGPT gpt-4 reranks only the top-30 of the gpt-3.5 ordering on BEIR ("†On BEIR, we use gpt-4 to re-rank the top-30 passages re-ranked by gpt-3.5-turbo"). R@100 of a reranker equals its first stage, so these rows do not belong in a first-stage Recall@100 comparison. BEIR BM25+CE (S3) reranks BM25 top-100 with a MiniLM-L6 cross-encoder.

**T10. Zero-shot claim vs training overlap.** BEIR is meant to be zero-shot (S3 abstract), but three of our datasets have training data in BEIR (S3 Table 1 training pairs: FiQA-2018 14,166; NFCorpus 110,575; SciFact 920). MTEB's own model metadata lists training sets per model (S10, `mteb/models/model_implementations/*.py`, read 2026-10-26): `voyage_models.py` `voyage-3-m-exp` set contains SciFact, NFCorpus, FiQA2018, ArguAna, TRECCOVID, QuoraRetrieval (regex-parsed; lines 937-998); `inf_models.py` lines 8-23 list all seven for `inf-retriever-v1`; `nvidia_models.py` `nvidia_training_datasets` contains ArguAna (line 54) and FiQA2018 (line 76); `nomic_models.py` line 133 lists QuoraRetrieval. gte models: `training_datasets=None` ("Not disclosed"). Whether the listed data is the train split or the test queries is not stated [INFERENCE: ArguAna/Quora have no train split in S3 Table 1, so overlap may include test material]. These rows are marked † in section 3.

**T11. FiQA-2018 BEIR test is a re-split of the original training data.** S3 Appendix D.2: "We randomly sample out 500 and 648 queries Q from the original training split as dev and test splits." Any model trained on the original FiQA training set may have seen BEIR's FiQA test queries [INFERENCE]. Large FiQA jumps (Qwen3-Embedding-4B/8B 0.627/0.646 vs bge-large 0.450, MTEB values in section 3) are consistent with that but I did not verify the Qwen3 training data.

**T12. MTEB dataset revisions differ across result files.** Across the 1,666 per-task files I fetched (238 models), `dataset_revision` differs: SciFact 0228b52 (131 files), d56462d (40), a75ae04 (9), not recorded (58); other tasks (majority revision / second revision / not recorded): NFCorpus ec0fa4f 171 / 7eb63cc 9 / 58; FiQA2018 27a1688 169 / 41b686a 9 / 60; ArguAna c22ab2a 177 / 5b3e369 9 / 52; SCIDOCS f8c2fcf 172 / 5c59ef3 9 / 57; TRECCOVID bb9466b 171 / 2c8041b 9 / 58; QuoraRetrieval e4e08e0 155 / 6205996 9 / 74. Current mteb main pins SciFact `d56462d0e63a25450459c4f213e49ffdb866f7f9`, NFCorpus `ec0fa4fe99da2ff19ca1214b7966684033a58814`, FiQA2018 `27a168819829fe9bcd655c2df245fb19452e8e06`, ArguAna `c22ab2a51041ffd869aaddef7af8d8215647e41a`, SCIDOCS `f8c2fcf00f625baaa80f62ec5bd9e1fff3b8ae88`, TRECCOVID `bb9466bac8153a0349341eb1b22e06409e78ef4e`, QuoraRetrieval `e4e08e0b7dbe3c8700f0daef558ff32256715259`. I did not diff the dataset contents, so whether the revisions are numerically equivalent is unknown. Each MTEB long-format row below carries its 7-char `dataset_revision`.

**T13. MTEB task = BEIR test split, main score nDCG@10 (verified).** In mteb main (2026-10-25) `sci_fact_retrieval.py`, `nf_corpus_retrieval.py`, `fi_qa2018_retrieval.py`, `argu_ana_retrieval.py`, `scidocs_retrieval.py`, `treccovid_retrieval.py`, `quora_retrieval.py` under `mteb/tasks/retrieval/eng/` all set `eval_splits=["test"]` and `main_score="ndcg_at_10"` (datasets `mteb/scifact`, `mteb/nfcorpus`, `mteb/fiqa`, `mteb/arguana`, `mteb/scidocs`, `mteb/trec-covid`, `mteb/quora`). I did not open the HF datasets to confirm that these repackagings equal BEIR's test qrels [INFERENCE]; the per-model values agree with Pyserini within rounding for bge-base (MTEB vs Pyserini: SciFact 0.744 vs 0.741, NFCorpus 0.374 vs 0.373, ArguAna 0.637 vs 0.636, SCIDOCS 0.217 vs 0.217, TREC-COVID 0.780 vs 0.781). All 1,666 fetched files used the `test` split.

**T14. Subset coverage.** ColBERTv2 reports only BEIR datasets "publicly-available" (S5 footnote 5); S6 reports 13 BEIR datasets; S7/S8 report 8/2. Averages over different subsets are not comparable (S4 section 6 says the same about the community's 11-15 dataset subsets).

**T15. Page-versus-doc deltas inside Pyserini.** SPLADE++ ED ArguAna 0.526 on the page vs 0.5218 in Anserini ONNX docs; BGE-base ArguAna 0.636 vs 0.6375 (section 2.2). BGE on ArguAna uses an empty query prefix on the page (`--query-prefix ""`), unlike the other six datasets.

## 7. Comparability legend (used in the long list)

- **N1** Pyserini/Anserini reference run: Lucene BM25 (k1=0.9, b=0.4) or learned model, `--remove-query`, `trec_eval -c`, R@100 uncapped.
- **N2** BEIR paper Table 2/9: pytrec_eval nDCG@10; neural models truncate at 512 word pieces; BM25 engine ambiguous (T7).
- **N3** BEIR paper TREC-COVID Recall@100 is capped recall (T4).
- **N4** ColBERTv2 paper: nDCG@10 only, public BEIR sets only.
- **N5** SPLADE-v3 paper: nDCG\* judged-only on some datasets (T6).
- **N6** Rerankers: rerank of a fixed top-100 first stage (T9).
- **N7** MTEB: pytrec_eval via mteb; identical-id removal only for ArguAna/FiQA2018/Quora in current task files (T3); `external` = model-card-imported file (T5, T12); † training overlap (T10).
